# ios-qemu (iOS 16 in QEMU) - working notes

**2026-10-22: project rebranded to ios-qemu (user decision).** Goal is now iOS 16.7 in QEMU/hvf on Apple Silicon; the phone is no longer a target (code kept, docs dropped: README rewritten, `site/` for hackiosx.dev removed from the repo; the live site is on Cloudflare and was not touched). Everything below about the phone is history.

## Agent handoff / active work (2026-10-15, latest)

### >>> Newer iOS / arm64e plan (2026-10-25, user decision; planning only, nothing built) <<<
Work happens on branch **`feature/arm64e`** (main stays the working iOS 16.7.16 iPhone X system).
- **Target: iOS 18.7.10 (22H374) on iPhone XS (iPhone11,2, D321AP)**, the last iOS for the XS (ipsw.me, still signed; signing is irrelevant, we only extract). Newest XS builds per major: 18.7.10 22H374, 17.7 21H16, 16.6.1 20G81 (no 16.7.x for XS). iOS 26 is out: it needs A13+.
- **Why XS:** same 1125x2436 @3x Face ID screen as the X (display/touch sizes, display-scale 3, home-button-type 2 carry over). A12 userland is arm64e.
- **PAC approach: arm64 kernel, PAC keys left off.** iOS 17+ userland is arm64e only; non-HINT PAC instructions (retab, blraa, braa, ldraa) must exist in the CPU (M-series has FEAT_PAuth/PAuth2/FPAC, checked via sysctl; TCG needs `-cpu max`, `pauth-impdef=on` for speed; the cortex-a76 default lacks PAC). With keys off sign/auth return the pointer unchanged. App Store apps are arm64; XNU already runs arm64 processes with user JOP keys disabled (`disable_user_jop`). Fallback if some Apple code checks PAC is really active (suspect: JavaScriptCore JIT in Safari): build the kernel arm64e (HAS_APPLE_PAC) with real keys.
- **Step 1 (probe): iOS 16.6.1 (20G81) XS userland on the current kernel source** (xnu-8796.141 = macOS 13.5 = the iOS 16.6 kernel) plus new patches: (1) accept/report CPU_SUBTYPE_ARM64E in exec grading (else launchd fails EBADARCH, errno 86), (2) arm64e shared-cache sliding (auth pointer format), (3) arm64e thread state (signed pc/lr flags) in signals/thread_set_state, (4) PAC keys disabled. Plus `mkadt.py --device x|xs` (XS: iPhone11,2/D321AP/D321, /product from the XS IPSW ADT, chip-id 0x8020, XS part number; IOPlatformUUID changes, A12+ UDID format CPID-ECID) and a new disk from the XS IPSW (research/iosroot/disk.img untouched). Board-named image tweaks (MediaToolbox D22/D221 configs) need D321 equivalents. Success = SpringBoard + Safari on the XS userland.

- **iOS 18 display: swap record layout (2026-10-25 ~19:50).** Observed on build 10 (`xnu/out-15-0006`, log `research/qemu-local/xs18-boot8.log`): backboardd swaps at 60 Hz (`SWEET FB: 1000 swaps` every 16.7 s, sel 5 input 1300 = 0x514 bytes) but SweetFramebuffer never logged a `swap N layer` line, so it saw no enabled layer and the screen kept the kernel console. Static RE of iOS 18 IOMobileFramebuffer (`ipsw dyld disass`, `--cache research/ipsw-xs18/dsc18.a2s`): `kern_SwapEnd` sends `obj+0x18`, size 0x514, sel 5 (record = object offset - 0x18); `kern_SwapBegin` stores the swap id at obj+0xb0; `kern_SwapSetLayer(ref, layer, surface, CGRect src, CGRect dst, flags)` stores the IOSurface id at obj+0xb4+4l, clipped int src rect at obj+0xc4+16l, enable=1 at obj+0x104+4l, int dst rect at obj+0x124+16l, ORs 1<<l into obj+0x164. Record offsets: swap id 0x98, ids 0x9c, src 0xac, enable 0xec, dst 0x10c, mask 0x14c = **every iOS 16 offset + 0x48** (iOS 16: 0x50/0x54/0x64/0xc4/0x104). Fix in **0079** (fixup, autosquashed, export verified against fresh 11417): SwapEnd with input >= 0x514 copies bytes 0x98.. to 0x50.. of the 0x46c iOS 16 layout; iOS 16 path unchanged. Local compile OK (xnu-cc.py, research/run15-4.log + research/bt15-1). CI `40227264124` -> `xnu/out-15-0007`. Our 0x3d never set bit 1 -> dyld ignored DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES entirely. **0115 fix:** policy 0x2b (@-paths, path vars, fallback, insert failures) + boot-arg `sweetdyldstrict=NAME` (no insert-failure leniency for that process). ### >>> SOUND (user request 2026-10-24, "only sound for now", own code, no hackios code) <<<
- **SOUND WORKS END TO END (2026-10-25 ~13:20-13:35, kernel 0108 = CI `40196425620` = `xnu/out-local` = `xnu/out-0108`, commit 8f9a6d9; harness/plugin/docs commit d3e1042).** Path: VirtualAudio's mix (QEMUAUDIO_TAP) -> QEMUAudio.c `tap_play()` (QEMUAUDIO_VSND, on): Float32 -> S16 stereo, `write()` to **/dev/qemuaudio** (O_NONBLOCK, 1024-frame chunks, reopen every ~2 s of audio if missing; only 44100 Hz output is passed on, else one log line) -> **0108** `bsd/dev/sweet_vsnd.c` (own code; boot-arg sweetvsnd=1): virtio-mmio sound device (ID 25, found at slot 28), VERSION_1 only, PCM_INFO -> first output stream with S16/44100/2 ch (QEMU: stream 0 out, stream 1 in, formats 0xe0078, rates 0x3fff), SET_PARAMS (period 2048 bytes = 512 frames, buffer 8 periods), PREPARE, START, stream left running; 16 KiB ring filled by write() (never blocks, drops what does not fit, whole frames), one kernel thread every 2 ms moves whole periods to the TX queue (max 8 in flight = ~93 ms; QEMU returns a TX buffer once its audio backend consumed it, so playback is paced by the host) and logs `SWEET VSND: N periods played, errors, bytes written, dropped` when playback has been idle for 1 s (never printed while an app keeps its IOProc running, e.g. an open WebAudio context streams silence). **harness.sh: audio is ON by default with --ios** (sweetvsnd=1 + `qemu-sweet.py --audio` = `-audiodev coreaudio,id=snd0 -device virtio-sound-device,audiodev=snd0`); **`--no-audio`** (user's flag name) leaves both out. Test recording: `./harness.sh --ios DISK -- --audio wav,path=OUT.wav` (last --audio wins; QEMU's wav backend records only what was played and leaves the header sizes 0 when killed, scratchpad wavcheck.py fixes them up). **Verified (observed):** boot 1 (wav, 110 s): chime peak 0.133 (-17.5 dB) in the WAV = the tap's value; 261 periods, 0 errors, 0 dropped. Boot 2 (wav, 420 s, UI driven with qemu-touch + scratchpad shot.py): WAV segments chime 0.133 / Settings -> Sounds -> Ringtone **Apex peak 0.244, ~4 s / Opening peak 0.356, ~7 s** / Safari WebAudio button (`data:text/html,<button ... onclick="c=new AudioContext();o=c.createOscillator();o.connect(c.destination);o.start();o.stop(c.currentTime+4)">PLAY</button>`; a `<script>function p(){...}` page rendered blank) **two 440 Hz tones, peak 1.0, rms 0.707, exactly 4.000 s each, no gaps**; 0 errors / 0 dropped in every kernel stats line; 220 s of audio recorded in ~200 s of wall time after the first tone (real-time pacing). Ringtone left at Opening (Default). Boot 3 (default = CoreAudio, 60 s, headless): VSND up, chime 259 periods played, 0 errors, 0 dropped (consumed by the Mac's output at real rate; not listened to by an agent). Boot 4: `--no-audio` -> no sweetvsnd boot-arg, no VSND lines. **Not verified: a human listening** (the user should run `./harness.sh --ios research/iosroot/disk.img` and listen for the chime at ~40 s). Known limits: volume buttons/Control Center routes do not apply (no real route, see option 1 below), playback latency up to ~190 ms (ring + in flight), clock drift between the guest's Null_Device clock and the host would show as drops (stats line) or underruns, none seen in these runs. Local kernel compile check: scratchpad xnu-cc-brew.py = tools/xnu-cc.py with Homebrew clang and the fork's CI path (`/Users/runner/work/ios-qemu/ios-qemu/dxb`); build tree of 0107 in research/bt107 (+ 0108 edits), originals research/pre0108, log research/run107.log.
Design: our own CoreAudio HAL driver `tools/guest/qemu-audio/QEMUAudio.c` (AudioServerPlugIn, loaded by mediaserverd from /System/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL) + later a kernel virtio-sound driver (0108, not started) + QEMU `-device virtio-sound-device -audiodev coreaudio` (Homebrew QEMU has both). Installer: `tools/audio-plugin.py setup|remove DISK` (also moves VirtualAudio.plugin back from HAL.disabled, where sweet-root-tweaks.py had put it in the phone days). Installed on the working disk 2026-10-24.
- Observed (reversed in VirtualAudio.plugin, stripped, otool -tV + own CFString decoder; scratchpad scripts): VirtualAudio classifies HAL devices by UID string only (factory compares "Codec", "Speaker", "USB Audio Output", ...). Codec requirements found one by one, each verified by a headless boot: input stream(s) (SubPortPolicy: "The HAL returned input stream size of %u"); input data sources ('ssc#' from a data source control) -> 'imic' becomes port pmbi "Built-In Microphone"; output ports from 'mute' controls on output elements: element 4 -> pspk "Built-In Speaker", element 3 -> prec "Built-In Receiver"; per-element volume controls (probes 'vdb#'). The plugin also wraps VirtualAudio's AudioObject* imports (QEMUAUDIO_TRACE_VA, diagnostics) to log every failing property query.
- Result so far (iteration 9, headless): no "No Audio Device Available"; VirtualAudioDevice_Default exists; ports Speaker/iPhone(receiver)/Built-In Mic exist. BUT the default VAD's route stays on Null_Device: VirtualAudio routed category 'csav' once at init (before the Codec appeared) and the later port additions do not re-route; the ConnectedToPower system sound at ~38 s played into Null_Device; no IO reached the plugin. Guess (unverified): an app activating an audio session re-routes. Next: test with app playback (user testing Settings ringtone previews 2026-10-25); if still Null, find what triggers RoutingHandler (or report the codec before VirtualAudio's first route).
- User test 2026-10-25 (with display): **YouTube now plays smoothly (video only, silent)**: it needed an audio device to exist (before: "No Audio Device Available"). No IO reached the plugin: everything still plays into Null_Device.
- Cause of the Null route (observed, tracer on VirtualAudio's __cxa_throw with backtraces): every route change fails with 'nort' (kVirtualAudioObjectRoutingNotSupportedError), thrown in VirtualAudio's property dispatcher (fn 0x121e4, sites 0x1e14c/0x1f604) when its routing-database lookup (fn 0x24bf0) returns status 1 for the requested category; e.g. Clock's alarm preview: "Route change failed: nort" (VirtualAudio_PlugIn.cpp:2100). The routing handler that ran at init is RoutingHandler_Playback_GenericConfig1 (log also shows RoutingSettings_N71 / 'Generic'): guess (unverified) the per-product database expects the D22 topology, e.g. the speaker on a separate "Speaker" device (the factory knows UID "Speaker"; D22 has a speaker amp), not on the codec.
- Tried and failed: QEMUAUDIO_AS_USB (USB transport, own UID): VirtualAudio's USB class needs 'lcID', which the HAL answers itself (from IOKit) and returns 'who?' even with 'lcID' in the custom property list. Switch kept, off.
- 2026-10-25 later (iterations 15-24, all headless, observed): plugin restructured as an object table; publishes "Codec" (receiver el. 3, mic 'imic', headphone jack control + el. 1 port, 'head' custom property {"earpiece design": 1} -> descriptor 'jb15') and "Speaker" (output + telemetry input, data sources 'spkr' / spV0 spI0 spV1 spI1; selections must be stored and announced, VirtualAudio waits 2 s for the change). All accepted without errors: ports Speaker (pspk), Built-In Receiver (prec), Built-In Microphone (pmbi), Wired Headphones (phpw, appears on the simulated jack insertion 8 s after start, in the forced 'fcru' update group).
- **Debug logging for VirtualAudio:** /Library/Preferences/Logging/Subsystems/com.apple.coreaudio.plist = {DEFAULT-OPTIONS: {Level: {Enable: Debug, Persist: Debug}}} on the working disk (diagnostic; VirtualAudio gates its levels 4/5 on os_log_type_enabled). Format strings are not decoded by ios-oslog.py for VirtualAudio, look them up by line number in the disassembly.
- **Still no audio reaches the plugin.** Routes are source->destination pairs through VirtualAudio's 'pap ' "Application Processor" port (conn 0, rout 0). The boot route pap->pnul (Null_Device) is computed before VirtualAudio adds other plug-ins' devices and is then served from RouteCache. Even the headphone insertion ends in RoutingManager.cpp:3223 "Port update completed without a route change (there is no supported route in the current category for the updated port(s).)"; every other category check logs RoutingManager.cpp:1898 "For %s: failed with result 1"; ringtones are also culled at :935 ("ringer switch is off", VirtualAudio's own ringer state). Guess (unverified): the database's routes need 'pap ' connected; what connects it is not found yet.
- **OPTION 2 STEP 1 WORKS (2026-10-25 ~12:45-13:00, user chose option 2 after the option 1 time box; 2 headless boots l27/l28, observed).** `QEMUAUDIO_TAP` (on) in QEMUAudio.c: VirtualAudio's imports AudioDeviceCreateIOProcID/Start/Stop are wrapped (same __got patch as the tracer). VirtualAudio plays the default VAD through ONE IOProc it creates on HAL device 83 **"VAD Aggregate Device"** (format lpcm 44100 Hz, 2 ch, Float32 interleaved, flags 0x9; 512-frame calls for system sounds, 128 for WebAudio), started per sound and stopped after it (AudioDeviceStart/Stop(83, 0x21)). The wrapper calls VirtualAudio's proc, then `tap_output()` reads the filled output buffer: that is the final mix (the aggregate's only sub-device is Null_Device, so nothing else consumes it). Measured in QEMUAudio.log (one line per 24000 frames): (1) boot ConnectedToPower chime: peak 0.13 (-17.5 dB), ~1 s; (2) **ringtones play**: Settings -> Sounds -> Ringtone, preview "Apex" peak 0.24, ~4.4 s, preview "Opening" peak 0.36, ~7 s (the QEMUAUDIO_RINGER fix keeps them from being culled); (3) app playback: Safari data: page with a WebAudio 440 Hz oscillator (tap starts it, 4 s): peak 1.0000, rms 0.707 (= a full-scale sine). YouTube not tried (needs network; the WebAudio test covers app playback). Headless UI driving: no console for screendump without --display; the scratchpad script shot.py pmemsaves the framebuffer (0x9c000000, 1125x2436x4) over QMP mon.sock. The ringtone setting was put back to Opening (Default). Unrelated observation: boot l27 had 24 `CRASH aggregated sig 9` lines, boot l28 none (not our process; not investigated).
- **NEXT (step 2):** hand `tap_output()`'s buffers (44.1 kHz stereo float; convert to what virtio-sound offers, e.g. S16 44100) to a kernel virtio-sound driver (patch 0108: virtio-mmio/pci sound device, one output PCM stream, a device node or IOKit user client the plugin writes to) + harness flag adding `-device virtio-sound-device,audiodev=snd0 -audiodev coreaudio,id=snd0` (Homebrew QEMU has both). Timing: VirtualAudio's aggregate is clocked by Null_Device's own timer, so expect drift vs the host clock; a ring buffer with drop/pad is enough at first. Option 1 (proper routes) stays open, see the attempt above.
- **Option 1 time-boxed attempt (2026-10-25 12:26-12:56, user-approved, 2 headless boots l25/l26, NOT solved):** observed: (a) product detection is right: hw.model D22AP, MGGetProductType 0x669eebc5, VirtualAudio product enum 16 (fn 0x5026c; 0 would be unknown), so "Generic" in the tuning paths is only the per-file fallback (`/Library/Audio/Tunings/D22/VAD` has only d22_*mic*/speaker *.austrip files, never loaded). (b) The ringer switch was OFF until SpringBoard posts `com.apple.springboard.ringerstate` at ~37 s; VirtualAudio culled pap->pspk for ringer-obeying categories (RoutingManager.cpp:935). New `QEMUAUDIO_RINGER` (on) sets that notify state to 1 from the plugin's Initialize: "Ringer switch state: on" at 1.5 s, no more culling lines. (c) Still no route: the first route (csav, 1.815 s) is computed before DeviceListener lists ap:ha.., Codec, Speaker (1.818 s); a late 'rtcf' csav re-route at 47/62 s (ringer on, all ports in, pspk conn 1 rout 1) gives the same concrete route pap->pnul ("Redundant concrete route(s)", routing handler not called again); a jack insertion at 52 s (ringer on) still ends in RoutingManager.cpp:3223 "no supported route"; every other category (cpar, cvcl, cvm, cspr, crng) still "failed with result 1". 'pap ' itself shows conn 0 rout 0 (also while crng candidates pap->pspk were produced, so probably not the blocker). Guess (unverified): the D22 routing database has no entries matching our ports/devices at all (only the Null fallback), i.e. it keys on something our devices lack (physical device type/descriptor, port subtypes, or speaker protection: RoutingHandler_Playback_GenericConfig1 has "HAL Speaker Protection is missing. Failing route %s", never logged because no speaker route reached the handler). Next for option 1: trace the DB lookup fn 0x24bf0 (status starts 2; strategies 0x1e78a8 / 0x1e7ea4 / 0x211d8 / 0x8132c set it; find which DB entries exist for product 16 and what each candidate is rejected for). Format strings of VirtualAudio log lines are at file offset = the `@0x...` in the log (decode with the VirtualAudio binary). ### >>> Video playback (user request 2026-10-23 ~16:35: "video playback without gpu") <<<

**YouTube FIXED (user, 2026-10-26): "Video unavailable" went away once an audio device existed (QEMUAudio + VirtualAudio, see SOUND: "YouTube now plays smoothly").** Earlier, parked by the user (2026-10-23 ~17:45): "no need to make youtube work for now". User saw YouTube's own "Video unavailable" often (with 0107). Not investigated; if resumed: reproduce, then read YouTube's + mediaserverd's os_log from an APFS clone of the disk (tools/ios-oslog.py) to tell a server-side refusal (sideloaded/modified app, device checks) from a local player failure.

**FIXED the decoder crash (2026-10-23 ~17:25): kernel 0107 = CI run after 0106 = `xnu/out-0107` (out-local still 0102).** Cause: SweetSurface's per-plane record (IOSurface create/lookup result, 0x80 bytes per plane from 0x68) had the plane's bytes-per-row at +0x08; IOSurface.framework reads it at **+0x10** (`IOSurfaceClientGetBytesPerRowOfPlane`; full layout from the client getters: width +0x00, height +0x04, offset +0x0c, bytesPerRow +0x10, size +0x14, bpe u16 +0x18, element w/h +0x1a/+0x1b, byte +0x54 = 1 means "no per-plane info, use surface fields"). So every multi-plane (video) surface had 0 bytes per row; the software H.264 decoder in mediaserverd then stepped its row pointer by -640 as u32 and wrote 4 GiB past the buffer (SIGBUS). Found with 0106 (crash log prints x0-x28: x7 = dst base, x19 = dst stride 0, x10 = x7 + 0x100000000). 0107 also writes +0x10 (keeps +0x08). Verified (run with VTTest, video-boot8.log): 420v planes 640/640 bytes per row; H.264 60/60 frames (0.6-0.9 ms/frame), HEVC 60/60 (0.4-0.5 ms/frame), no mediaserverd crash. YouTube Shorts now shows a real video frame (research/video-yt8a.png) instead of stripes, but two screenshots 8 s apart are almost identical (985 bytes differ): playback did not visibly advance; not investigated (paused? audio? buffering?). 0106 (register dump) stays: useful for every future crash.


User report: a sideloaded YouTube app plays no videos (CPU renderer). Not yet reproduced or logged. Static findings (research/dsc; helpers research/re/syms.py and research/re/dis.py, not in git):
- iOS 16 has SOFTWARE decoders in the shared cache: `/System/Library/VideoCodecs/H264SW.videocodec` (`VCPAVCRegisterDecoder`, H.264), `VCPHEVC.videocodec` (`VCPHEVCRegisterDecoder`, HEVC), `AV1SW.videodecoder`, `VCPMP4V`, `VCH263`, `AppleProResSWDecoder`; hardware: `AVD.videodecoder` (AppleAVD, H.264/HEVC/VP9), H264H*/HEVCH8/MP4VH*. No Info.plists on disk: `VideoToolbox VTLoadVideoDecoders` loads them by path + register symbol (H264SW first, AVD later).
- H264SW registers 'avc1'-family via `VTRegisterVideoDecoderWithInfo` with a one-entry info dictionary (keys/values are globals at H264SW+0x...960/968, not decoded yet): probably `kVTVideoDecoderInfo_RequiredSpecificationEntries` limiting it to certain clients (guess). Decoder choice: `VTSelectAndCreateVideoDecoderInstanceInternal`, `vtFilterRegistryItemByCodecTypeAndVideoDecoderSpecification`, `VTAvoidHardwareDecoders`, `VTGetAllowedDecoderList`, `vtPrintRegisteredVideoDecoders`; spec keys include `kVTVideoDecoderSpecification_AllowVP9Decode`, `_EnableAppleOnlySWAV1`, `_RequireSWAV1`, `_AllowParavirtualizedDecoder`.
- VideoToolbox also asks a paravirtualization host for decoders (`VTParavirtualizationGuestSendMessageToHostAndCopyReplySync`, registered as "paravirtualized:%@"): Apple's VM video path (needs the paravirtual device; parked with the GPU).
- **Measured 2026-10-23 ~16:50 (VTTest = `tools/guest/vttest/`, build.sh -> research/guest/VTTest.ipa, installed with install-app.py on clone `research/disk-video.img` of the working disk; kernel out-local 0102, CPU renderer):** VTIsHardwareDecodeSupported = 0 for avc1/hvc1/vp09/av01; `AVDRegister - AppleAVDCheckPlatform() returned FALSE` (hardware decoder correctly absent). VTDecompressionSession for the bundled H.264 and HEVC clips is created, but every frame fails with -12902 (kVTParameterErr) with and without EnableHardwareAcceleratedVideoDecoder=NO; AVAssetReader: -11819 (H.264) / -11821 + -12137 (HEVC). Forcing DecoderID `com.apple.videotoolbox.videodecoder.avc.vcp` (the software H.264 decoder; ids taken from macOS) -> session create -12911 (malfunction); `...hevc.vcp` -> -12906 (no such decoder on iOS 16). VTCopyVideoDecoderList is not exported on iOS (only its key constants). Control: the same code on the Mac decodes 60/60 frames on every path (research/vtmac/), so the test is right.
- **Cause found so far: decoding happens in mediaserverd, and the software H.264 decoder crashes there.** Each VTTest run is followed ~1 s later by `CRASH mediaserverd sig 10` (SIGBUS) in H264SW.videocodec on a decoder thread: a row-copy loop (str q0,[x10,x3] at H264SW+0x...c914 = _VCPAVCRegisterEncoder+0xf804) writes the decoded pixels to an address inside the shared cache's read-only text (far 0x2061d8040 / 0x203bd8040, region prot r-x, VM tag 35). So the decoder's destination buffer pointer is wrong in the VM; which buffer and why is not known yet. Disproved: the IOSurface per-plane flag theory (a 420v CVPixelBuffer in the app has valid plane addresses: offsets 64 / 230464), so patch 0106 was removed again. H264SW registration info is only {VTIsHardwareAccelerated: ...} (no client restriction); its StartSession always succeeds (reads pref ForceSyncH264SWDecoder in com.apple.coremedia).
- **Next:** dump mediaserverd's full registers at the crash (extend the kernel CRASH logger to x0-x28 for one process, or sweetsample on mediaserverd) to see the destination buffer (x7/x10) and where it comes from (IOSurface lookup across processes? pixel buffer pool attributes? a compressed/tiled output format?). YouTube itself: runs, Shorts shows a frame of vertical colour stripes and no motion (research/video-yt2.png), consistent with no working decoder.
- Plan: (1) a guest test app that decodes a small H.264 (and HEVC/VP9/AV1) clip with VTDecompressionSession and reports which decoder is chosen and the errors (+ vtPrintRegisteredVideoDecoders); (2) YouTube's own os_log while a video fails (tools/ios-oslog.py) to see which codec/stream it asks for; (3) then make the software decoders eligible (decode H264SW's required entries; patch the spec/allowed list, e.g. VTAvoidHardwareDecoders/defaults) or report "no hardware" so apps pick software paths.

Guest bundle `tools/guest/qemu-metal/` (QEMUMetal.m core: ring, heap, completion thread, constants recording; QMTLObjects.m: queue/command buffer/encoders/buffer/texture/library/function/pipelines/states/heap), protocol `qmtl.h`, host `tools/host/qemu-metal-host.m`, Mac self-test `tools/host/qmtl-selftest.sh` (PASS; draw step needs Xcode's Metal toolchain, not installed).
- Lessons (observed): MTLAddDevice asserts MTLDevice+MTLDeviceSPI conformance; init must always call [super init] (a nil return otherwise crashes in _MTLDevice dealloc); CA has no CPU fallback once a device exists; iOS constants class is MTLFunctionConstantValuesInternal and descriptors copy it; MPS (SpringBoard) aborts on our device, so only `backboardd` gets the device (process_allowed in QEMUMetal.m); host sets MTL_ERROR_MODE=nslog so Metal validation failures log instead of aborting; heap-full waits stalled backboardd ~1 s (now fail within ~20 ms); ivshmem does not exist in Homebrew QEMU on macOS (guest RAM is file-backed instead); shared memory is cached in the guest (memset 3.5-7.4 GB/s); pooled surfaces only for single-plane formats whose pixel size matches (planar/YUV in place gave a pink wallpaper).
- Performance (measured, scripted unlock + page swipes): 12-17 commits/s while animating; host commit->GPU done avg 1-6 ms (max ~20 ms); guest wait in commit avg 3-6 ms. So the remaining ~60 ms/frame is elsewhere; sampler snapshots (sweetsample=backboardd / SpringBoard) during swipes showed both idle in run loops. Not measured yet: the CPU renderer's frame rate for comparison; guest timer granularity of the usleep polling loops; panel copies capped at 30/s by sweetfb_hz=30.

**lockdownd over the network: accept check reversed (2026-10-22 ~21:25, lockdownd from the IPSW root 087-86698-044.dmg, stripped; llvm-objdump):** accept handler at 0x100021c18 classifies each connection: AF_UNIX (launchd socket /var/run/lockdown.sock) -> type 4 local; loopback TCP (127.0.0.1/::1) -> `getsockopt(IPPROTO_TCP, 0x204 = TCP peer pid)`: pid 0 (kernel peer, the device-side USB mux) -> type 1 USB, else "non kernel peer, pid %d!" + "Peer is unsafe." -> closed; any other TCP peer (our QEMU hostfwd arrives from 10.0.2.2) -> type 3 network, closed with "Killing the WiFi connection immediately." unless one of three data_ark booleans is true: domain `com.apple.mobile.wireless_lockdown`, keys `EnableWifiConnections`, `EnableWifiDebugging`, `EnableWifiPairing` (lookup 0x10000da24 -> data_ark key "%@-%@" = "com.apple.mobile.wireless_lockdown-EnableWifiConnections"; accepts CFBoolean true or nonzero CFNumber). lockdownd runs as root -> `/var/root/Library/Lockdown/data_ark.plist`. Experiment (user-approved): set EnableWifiConnections=true there on a clone of the working disk, connect with tools/ios-usbmuxd.py + `ideviceinfo`; pairing over a type-3 connection may additionally need EnableWifiPairing.

**GPU feasibility, virgl + own EAGL shim (2026-10-22 ~23:40, research only, nothing built):** ios-sailfish (github.com/joranv1/ios-sailfish, a fork of dipa-studio/ios-sweet for the Pixel 1, NO LICENSE -> do not copy its code; design ideas only) renders CoreAnimation on the Adreno 530 with Mesa freedreno: QuartzCore's GLES renderer (CA::OGL::GLESContext) instead of Apple's Metal-backed GLEngine. Their design notes: CA uses EAGLContext only via initWithAPI:properties:, API, setParameter:to:, getMacroContextPrivate, texImageIOSurface:..., attachImage:toCoreSurface:invertedRender:, +setCurrentContext:, +currentContext; every GL call goes through the GLI dispatch table from getMacroContextPrivate ({rctx, disp[1036]}, layout from GLEngine). They replace those methods for their own contexts, run Mesa surfaceless (CA draws into FBOs), import IOSurfaces as EGL images, and work around CA shader needs Mesa ES lacks: GL_TEXTURE_RECTANGLE, framebuffer fetch, missing tex_fragment_shaders entries (vibrant color matrix 0x2d, 0x14, variable blur 0x26/0x27). Injected into backboardd with DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES. Their GL layer: eagl.c ~77 KB, sweet_drm.c ~14 KB (libdrm/msm emulation), sweetgl.c ~9 KB, mesa.patch ~1.7 KB. They also confirm HEIC/video do not decode without the hardware decoder (they convert wallpaper HEIC to PNG: tools/wallpaper-heic2png.sh). For QEMU: same shim idea with Mesa virgl + our own XNU virtio-gpu (3D) driver + fake libdrm/virtgpu layer. Host: Homebrew QEMU 11.0.2 has NO OpenGL ("OpenGL support was not enabled in this build"; no virtio-gpu-gl), Homebrew has virglrenderer 1.3.0 -> needs a QEMU built with OpenGL + virglrenderer (UTM does this with ANGLE), untested. Own RE so far: QuartzCore renderer constructors gles_new_context 0x188440b4c, metal_new_context 0x188440b98, sw_new_context 0x1884409d0, pointers at __DATA_CONST 0x1d5b37c68/70/78; selection logic not traced (guess, unverified: CA falls back to SW when EAGL/GL context creation fails, so a working EAGL would make it pick GLES). User decision: write our own shim from scratch.

**IN-KERNEL LOCKDOWN MUX WORKS (2026-10-22 ~21:45): 0100 = CI `39849629084` = `xnu/out-local` (also `xnu/out-0100`).** `bsd/dev/sweet_mux.c` (sweetvnet=1): kernel thread listens on TCP 62079; per connection the host sends the device port (2 bytes BE), a kernel socket connects to 127.0.0.1:port, one status byte (0 / errno), then both directions are copied. qemu-sweet.py `--mux-port` (default 62079) forwards host 127.0.0.1:62079 to it (replaces the old --lockdown-port 62078 forward); tools/ios-usbmuxd.py no longer takes RUN_DIR and connects every device port through the relay (no more QMP hostfwd_add). Verified (run-20261022-214026, fresh clone `research/disk-mux.img` WITHOUT EnableWifiConnections): `SWEET MUX: listening on TCP 62079` (socket owner kernel_task:0); `idevice_id -l` -> a24fbc2a73a45ae3803c4a4139650b2f05f8f029 (real UDID with the QEMU identity); `ideviceinfo -s` unpaired returns UniqueDeviceID (USB-type connection; over the network path it was GetProhibited). Lock screen normal. **Next blocker: `idevicepair pair` -> error -12 (LOCKDOWN_E_MISSING_VALUE): `GetValue DevicePublicKey` -> MissingValue.** lockdownd has no device identity (key pair + DeviceCertificate); on a real device these come with activation (activation record / keychain item "lockdown-identities"; strings "Keychain not available, no key generation attempted.", `_load_cesm_certificate`). Our activation is the Hactivation branch (no record). lockdownd log arguments are <private>, so not yet known which source it tried.

**EnableWifiConnections experiment (2026-10-22 ~21:25, run-20261022-212340, disk `research/disk-lockdown.img` = clone of the working disk with data_ark key `com.apple.mobile.wireless_lockdown-EnableWifiConnections` = true, edited in place):** lockdownd now KEEPS host connections (via hostfwd 127.0.0.1:62078): QueryType -> com.apple.mobile.lockdown; unpaired GetValue returns basic keys (iPhone10,3, D22AP, 16.7.16) but `UniqueDeviceID`/`SerialNumber` -> GetProhibited (so tools/ios-usbmuxd.py lists no device; a scratch copy with a placeholder UDID gets further). `idevicepair pair` -> "Pairing is not possible over this connection" = PairingProhibitedOverThisConnection. Pair handler (around 0x1000290c8): if `[FMDFMIPManager sharedInstance] lockdownShouldDisableDevicePairing` is false, pairing requires connection type 1 (USB), otherwise refused; EnableWifiPairing only gates the separate SRP/PIN wireless pairing. => No data_ark setting makes normal pairing work over the network. Type 1 needs loopback TCP whose peer is a KERNEL socket (tcp_usrreq.c TCP_PEER_PID -> tcp_lookup_peer_pid_locked = 0), like Apple's device-side USB mux. Proposed: an in-kernel relay (kernel socket listens on en0, connects to 127.0.0.1:<port>, splices), ios-usbmuxd.py talks to it.

**Paravirtual Metal feasibility check (2026-10-23 ~01:00, research only, nothing built):** host QEMU side exists in stock Homebrew QEMU (`apple-gfx-pci`, Apple ParavirtualizedGraphics on the host GPU). Guest drivers ship with every macOS (this host, macOS 26): `/System/Library/Extensions/AppleParavirtGPU.kext`, `AppleParavirtGPUIOGPUFamily.kext`, `AppleParavirtGPUMetalIOGPUFamily.bundle` (Metal driver, NSPrincipalClass AppleParavirtDevice, v64.4.7), `AppleParavirtGPUMetal.bundle`, `AppleParavirtIOSurface.kext`, `AppleVideoToolboxParavirtualization.kext` (paravirtual video decode). Import check of the macOS 26 Metal bundle (arm64e slice, `xcrun dyld_info -imports`) against iOS 16 research/dsc: 193 imports, 191 present (Metal 55/55, IOGPU 34/34 incl. `_OBJC_IVAR_$_IOGPUMetalHeap._device` etc., libobjc, libc++/libc++abi, IOKit, CF, Foundation, IOSurface); missing only `_malloc_type_malloc` (newer macOS API, trivial stand-in) and `_memmove` (probably a resolver alias the scan does not list). A macOS 13 copy should match at least as well. Open problems: (1) bundle is arm64e-only, iOS 16 userland is arm64 -> needs a slice/cpusubtype rewrite and PAC left disabled in our kernel (untested); (2) the real cost is the kernel: IOGPU kernel interface used by IOGPU.framework + the paravirtual device protocol (reverse from AppleParavirtGPU.kext / AppleParavirtGPUIOGPUFamily.kext) + PCI discovery of apple-gfx-pci in our XNU; estimate weeks. Next cheap probe if resumed: list IOGPU.framework's kernel calls (IOConnectCall selectors) in iOS 16 and the classes/structure of the two macOS kexts. A `--apple-gpu` harness flag (adds apple-gfx-pci, macOS only, off by default) only makes sense once the kernel driver exists.

**Metal remoting milestone A WORKS: transport guest <-> host (2026-10-23 ~15:00, clone research/disk-metal.img, kernel 0104 = CI `39964090456` = `xnu/out-0104`, run-20261023-145716).** `./harness.sh --ios research/disk-metal.img --kernel xnu/out-0104/kernel.development.sweet --metal --secs 90` (bundle installed with `tools/metal-plugin.py setup DISK`). Observed: kernel `SWEET METAL: IOAcceleratorES registered (... 8 slots at 0xa0000000)`; every backboardd instance gets slot 0 (`SWEET METAL: slot 0 -> backboardd[56]`); host helper log (run dir `metal-host.log`): `slot 0: opened by backboardd[56] (generation 1)`, `hello from backboardd[56]`, `closed` when backboardd crashes (still the missing command queue), reopened with generation 3, 5, ...; guest QEMUMetal.log: `host answered in 1 ms (host generation N)`.
- Design: guest RAM is backed by a shared file (`qemu-sweet.py --metal FILE` = `-object memory-backend-file,...,share=on -M ...,memory-backend=ram`; harness puts it in the run dir as `guest-ram` and deletes it afterwards). The Metal region is guest PA 0xa0000000-0xb0000000 (boot-arg `sweetmetal_pa`), below phys_base 0x100000000 so the kernel never manages it (like the framebuffer at 0x9c000000). Host helper maps file offset PA-0x40000000. 8 slots x 32 MiB: 16 KiB header, 4 MiB ring, heap. Protocol: `tools/guest/qemu-metal/qmtl.h`.
- First attempt used QEMU `ivshmem-plain`: NOT available in Homebrew QEMU on macOS ("not a valid device model name"; ivshmem is Linux-only), so it was dropped (the stub change was reverted). Patch 0104's comment still talks about ivshmem and its default PA is 0x20000000; harmless because harness passes sweetmetal_pa=0xa0000000, fix the comment/default with the next kernel change.
- 0104: IOAcceleratorES maps the whole region into the kernel (kIOMapCopybackCache), user client selector 0 = attach (out: slot, user VA, size, generation), slot freed on clientClose/process death (generation +1). Host helper `tools/host/qemu-metal-host.m` (built by harness into research/qemu-local/qemu-metal-host) polls slot headers (200 us idle sleep), follows rings, answers HELLO/SYNC.
- **Next: milestone B** (objects + replay): command queue first (CA's "Failed to create MTLCommandQueue"), then command buffers, render encoders, textures (IOSurface contents copied through the slot heap at first), buffers in the slot heap (host wraps them zero-copy), pipelines from QuartzCore's metallib.

**Metal remoting step 1 booted (2026-10-23 ~14:45, clone research/disk-metal.img, kernel xnu/out-0103, `--bootargs "sweetmetal=1" --secs 120`; logs research/metal-boot{1,2,3}.log).** Observed:
- Kernel: `SWEET METAL: IOAcceleratorES registered`; backboardd loads QEMUMetal.bundle and calls `initWithAcceleratorPort:` (so discovery works end to end).
- Boot 1: backboardd abort in `MTLAddDevice`: it asserts `[newDevice conformsToProtocol:@protocol(MTLDevice)]` and `...MTLDeviceSPI` (then calls initLimits/initFeatureQueries). Fixed in the bundle with class_addProtocol at load.
- Boot 2/3: MTLAddDevice passes; only `featureProfile` and `maxBufferLength` reached the logger (now answered: 5 = `-[AGXA11FamilyDevice featureProfile]`, 256 MiB; IOGPU gets the real value from IOGPUDeviceGetMaxResourceSize). backboardd then SIGSEGVs in `CA::WindowServer::EAGLServer::renderer()`+0xe8: `CAMetalContextCreate` succeeded, `CA::OGL::new_metal_context` returned NULL and renderer() calls a virtual method on it. Reason (strings in new_metal_context): "Failed to create MTLCommandQueue" / "Mismatched MTLCommandQueue and MTLDevice": `newCommandQueue` is implemented by _MTLDevice itself (returns nil here), so it never reached the logger.
- **Important: once a Metal device exists, CoreAnimation commits to the Metal renderer; there is no fallback to the CPU renderer** (renderer() crashes instead). backboardd crash-loops (12x in 120 s), contextstored/siriactionsd abort too (other Metal users, not analysed). So the plug-in must stay behind `sweetmetal=1` until it works, and "log everything in one boot" does not work: each boot reveals the next object CA needs (next: a command queue whose `device` is ours, then command buffers, render pass/depth-stencil state, textures...).
- **Static answer to "public or private Metal?" (decision point of step 1):** QuartzCore's `objc_msgSend$` stubs (1412 selectors) intersected with every MTL* protocol method of the macOS 26 runtime give **142 Metal methods CA can call** (upper bound; list with protocols in `tools/guest/qemu-metal/ca-metal-selectors.txt`). Only 38 exist solely in SPI protocols, and most of those are constant capability queries on the device (maxColorAttachments, maxTextureWidth2D, supportsTileShaders/SIMDGroup/NativeHardwareFP16/MemorylessRenderTargets/RasterOrderGroups, alignment values, vendorName). Real SPI behaviour: newTextureWithBytesNoCopy:length:descriptor:deallocator:, newLinearTextureWithDescriptor:offset:bytesPerRow:bytesPerImage: (buffer-backed textures), setFragmentTexture:atTextureIndex:samplerState:atSamplerIndex:, commitAndWaitUntilSubmitted, command-queue priority/queues (no-ops), cache hints and resource groups (no-ops), pipeline library by name (newPipelineLibraryWithFilePath:, newRenderPipelineStateWithName:...; can likely return nil so CA compiles from default.metallib: unverified). Descriptor classes (render pass, pipeline, texture descriptors) are concrete classes inside Metal.framework, not driver work. Verdict: mostly public Metal; no AGX/IOGPU kernel interface needed.
- instrumentObjcMessageSends exists in iOS 16 libobjc but wrote no /tmp/msgSends-* file (not investigated). QEMUMetal.log is appended across boots (delete it between runs).

**Metal remoting step 1 built, NOT booted yet (2026-10-23 ~14:30).** Reversed iOS 16 device discovery (research/dsc, Metal): `MTLRegisterDevices` = IOServiceGetMatchingServices(IOServiceMatching("IOAcceleratorES")) -> per service `getMetalPluginClassForService` (IORegistryEntryCreateCFProperty `MetalPluginName` -> NSBundle `/System/Library/Extensions/<name>.bundle`, `MetalPluginClassName` -> classNamed:, must be isSubclassOfClass: `_MTLDevice`) -> `[[cls alloc] initWithAcceleratorPort:service]` -> `MTLAddDevice`. `-[_MTLDevice initWithAcceleratorPort:]` is abstract (doesNotRecognizeSelector:); `-[_MTLDevice init]` only sets up queues/library builder/serialization paths, no IOKit. So the kernel service is just a marker, no user client.
- **0103** (`xnu/patches/0103-sweet-metal-discovery.patch`, `iokit/Kernel/arm/SweetMetal.cpp`): built-in `IOAcceleratorES` on IOResources with MetalPluginName QEMUMetal / MetalPluginClassName QEMUMTLDevice, registered ONLY with boot-arg `sweetmetal=1` (logs `SWEET METAL: IOAcceleratorES registered`). CI `39960243784` passed -> `xnu/out-0103` (out-local still 0102).
- **Guest bundle** `tools/guest/qemu-metal/` (QEMUMetal.m + Info.plist), installed by `tools/metal-plugin.py setup DISK` (builds to research/guest/QEMUMetal.bundle, ad-hoc signed, copies to /System/Library/Extensions). Logging only: QEMUMTLDevice : _MTLDevice, init = [super init]; abstract methods (doesNotRecognizeSelector: overridden, no throw) and unknown methods (forwardInvocation:) are logged once per process and return nil/0; log in the guest's /private/var/tmp/QEMUMetal.log + os_log "QEMUMetal:".
- **Test clone** `research/disk-metal.img` has the bundle, BUT it was cloned while the user's VM was running on the working disk (crash-consistent; fsck_hfs passed). Prefer re-cloning with the VM stopped: `rm research/disk-metal.img; cp -c research/disk-lsrebuild2.img research/disk-metal.img; tools/metal-plugin.py setup research/disk-metal.img`.
- **Next:** boot `./harness.sh --ios research/disk-metal.img --kernel xnu/out-0103/kernel.development.sweet --bootargs "sweetmetal=1" --secs 120`, then read QEMUMetal.log from the clone (and ios-oslog `--grep QEMUMetal`). Expected risk: with a Metal device present CoreAnimation may pick the Metal renderer and get nil objects -> black screen or crash loop in backboardd (clone only). Decision point: mostly public MTLDevice methods -> build the host helper (Objective-C, shared guest RAM); heavy private AGX/IOGPU use -> park.

**GPU without emulating a GPU: Metal API remoting over shared guest RAM (2026-10-23 ~14:20, research + probes, nothing built; the user asked for other ways after the paravirtual route looked hard).** Idea: guest and host are both Apple platforms with Metal, so instead of a virtual GPU device, a guest Metal driver bundle forwards Metal objects/commands to a host process that runs them on the real GPU, over a protocol we design (no Apple protocol to reverse, no QEMU patch). Three probes, all positive (observed):
- **Plug-in path:** iOS 16's Metal finds drivers through an IOKit accelerator service's `MetalPluginName` / `MetalPluginClassName` properties and loads `/System/Library/Extensions/%@.bundle` (strings in research/dsc; backboardd already polls IOAcceleratorES/AGXAccelerator, see the 0053s milestone). So: our kernel publishes a stub accelerator service with those properties, our bundle provides the MTLDevice class (Metal is protocol-based). Selection logic in Metal.framework not traced yet.
- **Shared memory with stock QEMU + hvf:** `-object memory-backend-file,id=mem,size=1G,mem-path=FILE,share=on -M virt,memory-backend=mem` works under hvf; a separate host process that mmaps FILE sees guest RAM live (a guest store loop at 0x40300000 was visible changing; guest PA 0x40000000 = file offset 0). Gives a zero-copy channel (command ring in guest RAM) and lets the host wrap guest IOSurfaces (SweetSurface wired buffers; we can make them physically contiguous) as MTLBuffer/texture memory without copies. Doorbell: polling first; later a virtio device or a chardev socket.
- **iOS 16 shaders on host Metal:** QuartzCore's `default.metallib` from the iOS 16.7.16 root (141 functions: 39 vertex, 95 fragment, 7 kernel) loads with `newLibraryWithURL:` on macOS 26 and the M5 compiler (AGXMetalG17G) builds real pipelines: 33/95 fragment functions compiled in a blind vertex/fragment pairing, 3/7 kernels. Every failure was a setup error of the blind test, not a format problem: interface mismatch with the random vertex function (69), missing vertex descriptor (48), framebuffer-fetch shaders without a real colour attachment (23), and 4 tile_downsample kernels that must be built as tile pipelines (`air.load.implicit_imageblock`). CoreImage.metallib also loads (my test tripped a validation assert on its function types; not followed up). Test sources were in the session scratchpad (Swift, ~40 lines: makeLibrary + makeRenderPipelineState over all pairs, zero-specialized function constants).
- **Prior art:** the iOS Simulator does the same layering: `MTLSimDriver.framework` in the installed iOS 26.5 simruntime forwards Metal from the simulated process to the host (over XPC; not reusable directly, but a reference for which Metal surface CA needs).
- **Cost/risk (estimate):** guest bundle implementing the Metal subset CA's MetalContext uses (device, queue, command buffer, render/blit encoders, textures incl. IOSurface-backed, buffers, pipelines from QuartzCore's metallib, samplers, fences/events) + host app (Swift, same objects on real Metal) + a small kernel part (stub accelerator service, physical addresses of IOSurfaces, maybe a doorbell). Unknown-method logging (forwardInvocation) shows exactly which calls CA makes. Risks: CA may need AGX/IOGPU-private methods or MTLDevice properties beyond the public protocol; arm64 guest bundle against iOS 16 Metal private classes; synchronisation/latency of polling. Grows toward app Metal later. Display: CA renders into IOSurfaces that our SweetFramebuffer already scans out, so no new display path is needed.
- **Other routes considered (not probed):** GLES remoting with Google's gfxstream (mature macOS host support via ANGLE/MoltenVK; guest needs our EAGL shim like the virgl idea plus a transport; CA's GLES path needs extensions such as framebuffer fetch); Venus (Vulkan over virtio-gpu) to KosmicKrisp/MoltenVK plus zink in the guest (needs a QEMU with virtio-gpu-gl/rutabaga and our own virtio-gpu driver); remoting CoreAnimation transactions to a host QuartzCore (iOS 16 vs macOS 26 protocol mismatch, rejected).

**Apple Paravirtual GPU research (2026-10-23 ~13:50, research only, nothing built; artifacts in a session scratchpad, re-fetchable with the commands below):**
- **Kernel ABI match (observed):** iOS 16.7.16 (iPhone10,3 20H392) kernelcache has `com.apple.iokit.IOGPUFamily` **65.60.1**, the macOS 13.5 VirtualMac kernelcache (VirtualMac2,1 22G74, `root:xnu-8796.141.3~6/RELEASE_ARM64_VMAPPLE` = our XNU source and template) has the **same 65.60.1**. So iOS 16 IOGPU.framework talks to the same IOGPUFamily user-client ABI as macOS 13.5, and that kernelcache's `com.apple.driver.AppleParavirtGPUIOGPUFamily` 13.4.13 (289 KB) + `com.apple.iokit.AppleParavirtIOSurface` 13.4.13 (112 KB) + IOGPUFamily (545 KB) are a ready reference for an own driver. Fetch: `ipsw download ipsw --device VirtualMac2,1 --build 22G74 --kernel` then `ipsw kernel extract KC BUNDLE_ID`; iOS: `ipsw download ipsw --device iPhone10,3 --build 20H392 --kernel`. The host's own kernelcache (Preboot) has no paravirt kexts, and `/System/Library/KernelCollections/*.kc` on this Mac are x86_64 only.
- **Guest-side device (observed, VM device tree `DeviceTree.vma2macosap.im4p`):** on Apple Silicon the GPU is NOT PCI. ADT nodes under arm-io: `gfx` {compatible `paravirtualizedgraphics,gpu`, role APV_GFX, reg 0x20200000+0x10000, interrupts 1} and `iosurface-mapper` {compatible `paravirtualizedgraphics,iosurface`, role APV_IOSFC, reg 0x20210000+0x10000, interrupts 0}. Kext personalities: AppleParavirtGPU (IOAccelerator category, IONameMatch `paravirtualizedgraphics,gpu`, provider AppleARMIODevice, MetalPluginName AppleParavirtGPUMetalIOGPUFamily / class AppleParavirtDevice); IOSurfaceParavirtMapperDevice (IONameMatch `paravirtualizedgraphics,iosurface`). The PCI kext (`AppleParavirtGPU.kext`, IOPCIMatch 0xEEEE106B, IOAcceleratorFamily2/IOGraphicsFamily) is the Intel-guest driver. Our SweetPlatformExpert already makes nubs for /arm-io children, so mkadt.py can add these two nodes.
- **Kext structure (strings, release kext so no symbols):** classes AppleParavirtGPU (setupMMIO/setupVersion/setupRoot/setupFIFO/writeFifo/setupInterruptsEarly/handleFaultInterrupt/configureDevice/DisplayPortCount), Root/VirtualChannel (FIFO command rings), CommandDescriptor/CommandQueue/WorkQueue/EventMachine (stamps + barriers), Device + DeviceUserClient (createObject/deleteObject/createFunction/createComputePipeline/heaps), Resource/ResourceHeap/SysMemory/MemoryMap/PageTable (guest page tables handed to the host), MapperMemory (IOSurface mapper), Task/KernelTask, and **AppleParavirtDisplay** (an IOMobileFramebuffer-style display: IOMFBUUID, cursor plane, gamma, swap fences, shared-state page). Mapper kext: IOSurfaceParavirtMapperDevice builds per-surface page tables (plane count/size/compression checks) and sends them to the host. Interesting for us: the guest display could become AppleParavirtDisplay (QEMU shows PG frames itself) instead of our SweetFramebuffer.
- **iOS 16 userland (observed):** the shared cache has IOGPU.framework and `VideoToolboxParavirtualizationSupport.framework` (PROJECT:AppleParavirtGPU-1), and IOMobileFramebuffer counts `paravirtualizedgraphics,gpu` nodes as displays (see the IOMFB entry below), i.e. Apple had virtualized iOS 16 with this GPU internally; but there is **no paravirt Metal driver bundle** in the iOS 16 cache. Source for it: macOS 13.5's AppleParavirtGPUMetalIOGPUFamily.bundle (in the macOS 13.5 shared cache; pairs with the 13.4.13 kext; not downloaded yet) or this host's macOS 26 copy (import check in the entry above: 191/193 resolve against iOS 16).
- **Host side (observed):** Homebrew QEMU 11.0.2 has only `apple-gfx-pci`; it has no `vmapple` machine and no `apple-gfx-mmio` (Kconfig: MAC_PVG_MMIO is selected only by VMAPPLE; sysbus device, not user-creatable). QEMU's `hw/display/apple-gfx-mmio.m`: region 0 = PGDevice MMIO, region 1 = PGIOSurfaceHostDevice MMIO, two IRQs, `usingIOSurfaceMapper = true`; vmapple maps them at 0x30200000/0x30210000 (IRQ 0x11/0x10). Needed: our own QEMU build with a small patch to put apple-gfx-mmio on `virt` (plus ADT nodes at the chosen addresses). PG protocol is inside Apple's ParavirtualizedGraphics.framework, so the guest register/FIFO protocol must come from reversing the kext. vmapple docs say only macOS 12 guests are supported by QEMU (boot reasons); PG itself accepts older guest drivers (Virtualization.framework runs macOS 12+ guests).
- **Options (assessment, unverified):** (A) own built-in drivers (project style): IOGPUFamily subset for IOGPU.framework + AppleParavirtGPU protocol + IOSurface mapper hooked into SweetSurface, reversed from the 13.5 kexts; weeks. (B) load Apple's binary kexts: needs a kext-collection loader in our kernel, AppleARMPlatform's AppleARMIODevice provider class and Apple's IOSurface.kext (clashes with built-in SweetSurface); judged harder than A. Userland in both: the macOS Metal bundle rewritten arm64e -> arm64 (PAC keys off in our kernel, so PAC instructions are no-ops; untested) plus a Metal compiler path (unknown how the bundle hands shaders to the host; check next).
- **PCI probe step 1 (2026-10-23 ~14:05, observed):** stock Homebrew QEMU 11.0.2 creates `apple-gfx-pci` on our machine type without errors (`qemu-system-aarch64 -M virt,gic-version=3,highmem-ecam=off -accel hvf -cpu host -m 2G -S -display none -device apple-gfx-pci,addr=0x2 -monitor stdio`, no guest code run): `info pci` shows bus 0 device 2 "Display controller: PCI device 106b:eeee", subsystem 1af4:1100, BAR0 32-bit memory + BAR6 (ROM), both unmapped until the guest programs them. So the host PG device realizes under `virt` on this Mac (macOS 26, M5) with no QEMU patch. Not yet known: whether it answers register reads from an ARM guest. Step 2 (not started): a small kernel probe that programs BAR0 via ECAM like the UFS path and prints the first registers (version register, see setupVersion). Note: the PCI variant runs without the IOSurface mapper (`usingIOSurfaceMapper` off), so surface sharing would follow the Intel-guest driver (x86_64 AppleParavirtGPU in this Mac's SystemKernelExtensions.kc); the apple-gfx-mmio QEMU patch remains the preferred long-term route.
- **PCI probe step 2 (2026-10-23 ~14:10, observed, no kernel change):** `tools/apple-gfx-probe.S` (bare-metal guest program loaded instead of our kernel, usage in its header) programs BAR0 of 00:02.0 and reads it. Result: BAR0 = **16 KiB** 32-bit memory BAR (mapped at 0x10000000; QEMU trace `apple_gfx_common_init ... MMIO size: 16384`), and every ARM guest load under hvf reaches Apple's PGDevice (one `apple_gfx_read` trace line per load, no errors, no faults). Of all 4096 32-bit registers only **offset 0x122c reads 1**, everything else 0, so the version/setup handshake is not a plain readable register; it needs the driver's write sequence (setupMMIO/setupVersion in the kext). Default display modes listed by QEMU: 1920x1080, 1440x1080, 1280x1024. Next: reverse setupMMIO/setupVersion in the 13.5 ARM kext (string xrefs; `llvm-objdump` on the extracted kext gives no symbols, the 0x18xx immediates seen are object field offsets, not MMIO) and replay its first writes in the probe while tracing `apple_gfx_*`.
- **Next cheap probes:** (1) IOGPU.framework's IOConnectCall selectors in iOS 16 (`ipsw dyld` on research/dsc takes minutes per query; use the dsc-a2s + llvm-mc method) and IOGPUDeviceUserClient's dispatch table in the 13.5 IOGPUFamily (identical); (2) the macOS 13.5 Metal bundle and how it compiles shaders; (3) a QEMU build with apple-gfx-mmio on virt, booting our kernel with the two ADT nodes and logging the first MMIO reads (version register) to see the device answer.

First hvf boot halted after EARLY 8: lldb on QEMU's gdbstub (hw breakpoints work under hvf) showed a level-2 translation fault on the first write to the early-fb alias 0xfffffe0002000000, with page tables identical to TCG (stale walk cache from the bootstrap TTBR1). **0082** adds `dsb ishst; tlbi vmalle1is; dsb ish; isb` in sweet_early_relocate (first try used ';' -> only dsb emitted; Apple asm comment char again). Kernel phase timings hvf vs TCG: 0.017 s vs 0.168 s to PMRD, 0.037 vs 0.29 s after.
- **0083**: IOKitInitializeTime no longer waits 30 s for IORTC (no RTC driver; phone also lost 30 s per boot). QEMU test init now runs ~5 s after start.
- Screen console is a circular buffer: the tail of fb.txt is not the newest text. Use the msgbuf (`_msgbufp` -> struct msgbuf, `--hmp "memsave VA SIZE research/x.bin"`, no '-' in the path) for the real order.
- **Root over emulated UFS, no new driver**: `qemu-sweet.py --ufs DISK` adds QEMU's `ufs` PCI device at 00:01.0 (`highmem-ecam=off`, `-nic none`) + `ufs-lu`; the stub (with -DUFS_BAR) assigns BAR0 0x10000000, enables bus mastering, sets HCE and runs DME_LINKSTARTUP (what ABL does on the phone). `mkadt.py --qemu-ufs` puts /sweet-ufs reg=0x10000000. `tools/mkufsdisk.py` writes the 4 KiB-sector GPT ("userdata", +8 MiB for the 0047 log). Verified with a 64 MB HFS+ test root (newfs_hfs, sweet-init as /sbin/launchd): `SWEET UFS: "userdata" on LUN 0` -> `BSD root: ufsroot0` -> init runs (`research/qemu-local/testdisk.img`).
- RAM disk is not an option for the real root: mkpayload puts it inside the kernel's static region (highmem layout ~3.95 GiB total); the root is ~10.7 GiB uncompressed (OS 7.5, SystemOS 3.1 of which the shared cache is 3.1).
- IPSW `research/ipsw/iPhone10,3,iPhone10,6_16.7.16_20H392_Restore.ipsw` (sha1 verified by ipsw); OS 087-86698-044.dmg, SystemOS 087-86593-047.dmg, AppOS 087-86715-047.dmg extracted to `research/ipsw/x/`. `research/ipsw-x/d22ap.adt` extracted (mkadt /product now present).
- `tools/mkiosroot-mac.sh` (needs sudo + `brew install rsync`): macOS port of mkiosroot.sh + sweet-root-tweaks.py -> OUT/root.hfs + OUT/disk.img. NOT RUN YET.
- `./harness.sh [--hvf] [--ios DISK]`: --ios uses the phone's boot-args with rd=ufsroot0 over emulated UFS.
- NEXT: run mkiosroot-mac.sh, then `./harness.sh --ios research/iosroot/disk.img --secs 300` and follow launchd -> backboardd -> SpringBoard.

**Kernel workflow:** edit `research/bt28/xnu/...` (keep the pre-edit copy in `research/preNNNN/`), syntax check `python3 tools/xnu-cc.py research/run28.log research/bt28 <file>`, make `xnu/patches/NNNN-*.patch` with `diff -u pre bt28 | sed` path rewrite, add new/changed files to `research/check-patch-order.py` list, run it (must say applied + MATCH), commit + push -> GitHub Actions `xnu` builds (~8 min) -> `gh run download <id> -R dipa-studio/ios-sweet -n xnu-kernel -D xnu/out-NNNN`. **Repo is PUBLIC since 2026-10-17** (private macOS runner minutes ran out: "recent account payments have failed"); public = free runners. Commit trailer: only `Co-Authored-By: dipa-studio <183933817+dipa-studio@users.noreply.github.com>`.

**Phone workflow (user does buttons, Claude runs commands):**
- Boot test kernel: phone in fastboot -> `fastboot boot shim/out/shim-boot-X.img`. NEVER `fastboot getvar`.
- **Read logs WITHOUT filming:** after a test the user holds Power+VolDown -> fastboot -> `fastboot boot recovery/recovery.img` (OrangeFox, temp) -> wait for `adb shell true` -> `tools/sweet-log-pull.sh /run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/logs/<name>.txt` (reads the 4 MiB console ring the kernel keeps in the last 1025 blocks of userdata; patch 0047). Then `adb reboot bootloader` brings the phone back to fastboot without the user.
- **Flash a root image:** `fastboot flash -S 512M userdata <img>.simg` (7 min, 25 chunks). After a big flash the Xiaomi ABL fastboot hangs: next `fastboot boot` stalls at "Sending" -> the USER must re-enter fastboot by hand (Power+VolDown). Do not retry commands against the hung ABL. A flash right after `adb reboot bootloader` once failed on chunk 1 (USB protocol error): just retry. User must NOT unplug during flash (once interrupted at 25/25 -> reflash).
- **Edit the phone root without reflashing (same-length only):** find the file's raw offset in the local image (it is one extent; search the image for its first 4 KiB), compare with `adb exec-out dd if=/dev/block/sda34 ...`, then `adb push` changed 4 KiB blocks and `dd of=/dev/block/sda34 seek=... conv=notrunc,fsync`, read back. Recovery kernel has no hfsplus.
- **Pull the whole phone root** (12 min): `adb exec-out "dd if=/dev/block/sda34 bs=1048576 count=16384" > img`; mount ro with udisksctl or `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,ro`. To write into a pulled image: `sudo fsck.hfsplus -fy` first (volume is never cleanly unmounted -> Linux mounts ro even with -o force), then `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,force`.
- Making images: copy latest img with `cp --sparse=always`, mount rw with force, change, `sync; umount`, `sha256sum`, `img2simg`. Keep only the latest image + one fallback on the SSD (/home is ~98% full; big work files live in `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/`, old kernels/images archived in `.../ios-sweet-work/archive/`).

**Diagnostics built into the kernel (all SWEET-only):** persistent console log (0047, boot-arg sweetlog=0 off; flush every ~1 s); `|name| text` copies of /dev/null writes for processes in `sweetnull` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0048); `IOK name:` IOKit lookup/open trace for `sweetiolog` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0049/0054, 400 lines max); `SWEET Sandbox: proc op N` (0050); `STR ...` heap text dump on SIGABRT/SIGTRAP for `sweetscan` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0051/0053) - this is how NSException/BSAssert reasons were found; `SMP name tN pc .. bt ..` user stack sampler for `sweetsample` (default SpringBoard,backboardd; first at 90 s then every 120 s, 40 samples, 16 frames; 0054/0055); `SWEET FB: N swaps` every 1000 swaps; `CRASH name ...` crash reports (0043/0044). Documentation only; no kernel build, tests, flashing or phone boot performed.

- **v11 still migrated (boot 0056u, 20:32 UTC, `logs/boot-0056u-v11.txt`).** On iOS kCFPreferencesCurrentHost lands in the plain plist: all DMEnvironment setters (setLastBuildVersionPref, setUserDataDispositionPref, ...) use CurrentHost and the phone's `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` holds DMUserDataDisposition; datamigrator's XPC service runs with `_RoleAccount: mobile`. **Image v12** = v11 + `LastSystemVersion` = 20H392 added to that plain `com.apple.migration.plist` (keeps DMUserDataDisposition, 501:501 0600) + `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin` moved to `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL.disabled/` (Codex: stops the mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop; untested). **installcoordinationd loop explained (Codex `codex/installcoordinationd-report.md`):** os_crash "Failed to create data directory" EACCES for `/private/var/containers/Shared/SystemGroup/systemgroup.com.apple.installcoordinationd/Library/InstallCoordination`: `Library`/`Library/Caches` in the container are ACL-only (uid/gid 0xfffffffe, mode 0000, ACE for group GUID ...012B = gid 299 systemusers); with `config_ext_resolver` the kernel asks an identity resolver (memberd/opendirectoryd) that iOS does not run -> EWOULDBLOCK -> ACE skipped -> EACCES. **Patch 0057**: drop config_ext_resolver from FILESYS_BASE (compatibility UID/GID<->GUID mapping is used instead), likely fixes other ACL-protected containers too. Codex task 3: UFS driver performance design (`codex/ufs-performance.md`).
- **mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop explained (Codex report `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/codex/mediaserverd-report.md`):** the fault (FAR 0x390) is in the HAL plug-in `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin/VirtualAudio` (ApplyBeamFormerPreference <- VirtualAudio_PlugIn::SetPropertyData <- HALS_AHPPlugIn::ObjectSetPropertyData <- AudioObjectSetPropertyData <- MediaExperience vaemVADSerializationListener): the default virtual audio device ('vdef', VirtualAudioDevice_Default) does not exist (no audio hardware/driver, no /product/audio). Proposed workaround for GUI bring-up: move VirtualAudio.plugin out of /Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/ in the next root image (untested). v11 reflashed OK after an interrupted flash (USB unplugged at chunk 25/25). Codex task 2: installcoordinationd crash loop.
- **ROOT CAUSE of the endless migration: no IOPlatformUUID (2026-10-17).** datamigrator's `-[DMMigrator isMigrationNeededWithBuildVersion:lastBuildVersion:clientPID:]` gets lastBuildVersion from `-[DMEnvironment lastBuildVersionPref]` = `CFPreferencesCopyValue("LastSystemVersion", "com.apple.migration", kCFPreferencesCurrentUser, kCFPreferencesCurrentHost)` -> a ByHost file. The phone had `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` (only DMUserDataDisposition) and NO `Preferences/ByHost` directory at all. On arm XNU publishes IOPlatformUUID (gethostuuid) only from `/chosen/unique-chip-id` (8 bytes) + `/chosen/chip-id` (4 bytes) (IOPlatformExpertDevice::generatePlatformUUID, called from IOPlatformExpert::start); the SWEET ADT had neither, so every CurrentHost preference write failed. Fix: mkadt adds `unique-chip-id` = 0x5357454554000001 (synthetic) and `chip-id` = 0x8015 -> UUID `A9683613-3704-53F2-95D0-98A8FB574E78` (same SHA1 namespace computation as the kernel). ADT `shim/out/sweet-0057hi.adt`, boot image `shim/out/shim-boot-0056u.img` (0056 kernel + 0057hi ADT, QEMU OK). **Image v11** = v10 + `Preferences/ByHost/com.apple.migration.<UUID upper and lower>.plist` {LastSystemVersion: 20H392} (501:501, dir 0755 files 0600). Flashing v11 2026-10-17 ~20:10 UTC. Codex (gpt-6.1-sol high) analysing the mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop in `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/codex/` (report `mediaserverd-report.md`).
- **v10 did NOT stop the migration (boot 0056n on v10, 19:18 UTC, `logs/boot-0056n-v10.txt`):** datamigrator + migrationpluginwrapper ran again, backboardd drew the progress UI (13000+ swaps in ~5 min). So `com.apple.DataMigration.plist`/LastSystemVersion is not the deciding input. What is known about the decision (datamigrator XPC service `DataMigration.framework/XPCServices/com.apple.datamigrator.xpc/com.apple.datamigrator`, strings): `Migration sentinel present. Migration necessary.` (sentinel `/private/var/datamigrator/migrate`, created and removed by datamigrator itself; absent in v10), `System build version changed from %@ to %@. Migration necessary.` / `...unchanged... not necessary`, `Disposition dictionary with build version %@ is out of date relative to current build version %@`, `SyntheticDidUpgrade`, DMUserDataDispositionManager; previous build source not found yet (not in the binary's plain strings; likely in DataMigration.framework's DMUserDataDispositionManager / preferences domain `com.apple.migration`). backboardd calls `DMPerformMigrationIfNeeded` and holds SpringBoard in `checkInAndWaitForDataMigration` until it returns. NEXT: find where the previous build version / disposition dictionary is stored (disassemble DataMigration.framework DMUserDataDispositionManager + datamigrator's isMigrationNeeded path), write it into the image; or get datamigrator's os_log out (OS_ACTIVITY_DT_MODE needs a launchd env change).
- **Data migration never records completion -> skip it (image v10, 2026-10-17).** Boot 0056n (18:16 UTC, `logs/boot-0056n.txt`, 30 min): 10 Hz panel copies, backboardd still presents at ~60 Hz (107k swaps), SpringBoard still in `BKSSystemShellService _checkIn` at sample 14. Every migration run takes 1+ hour on cpus=1 and repeats each boot. DataMigration decides from `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.DataMigration.plist` key `LastSystemVersion` (vs ProductBuildVersion 20H392); the pulled phone root (21:01 local) had NO such file. **v10** = that pulled phone state + fsck.hfsplus -fy (volume was marked not cleanly unmounted -> Linux mounts it read-only even with -o force; fsck found only hard-link counts + minor volume header, repaired) + `com.apple.DataMigration.plist` {LastSystemVersion: 20H392} (501:501 0600). `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-ipsw/ios16-root-20H392-v10.img` (+ .sha256, .simg); v8 files deleted, v9 kept as fallback.
- **Repo made PUBLIC (2026-10-17, user request) so GitHub-hosted macOS runners are free:** the 0056 CI run was refused ("recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased"). Checked before: no device dumps, serials, IMEI or Apple binaries in the history (.gitignore keeps backup/, dtb/, recovery/, research/ out); commit author emails are public now. 0056 rerun `39056660104` passed -> `xnu/out-0056`, QEMU OK, image `shim/out/shim-boot-0056n.img` booted 18:16 UTC.
- **Boot 0055n (17:16 UTC, ~65 min, `logs/boot-0055n.txt`): migration bar reached 99% in ~30 min (was ~60), then crawled.** Root cause of the slowness: backboardd presents the progress UI at ~58 Hz (**167,000 swaps in ~48 min**) and AppleCLCD copied the full 10 MB frame into the panel on every swap -> ~600 MB/s memcpy on the single CPU (cpus=1), starving everything (polled UFS I/O too). Sampler (16 frames): **SpringBoard waits in `-[BKSSystemShellService _checkIn]`** (synchronous BSXPC call to backboardd, from FBSystemShellInitialize <- SBSystemAppMain) - backboardd holds the system shell check-in until the data migration ends, so the migration is the gate to SpringBoard UI. **Patch 0056**: swap only records the latest swap record; the vsync timer copies it to the panel every 60/sweetfb_hz ticks (default 10 Hz); `SWEET FB: N swaps` every 1000.
- **Boot 0054n (16:53 UTC, ~12 min, `logs/boot-0054n.txt`, patch 0054 sampler):** the data migration runs AGAIN on every boot (it completed once in the 0053s run) - completion is not being recorded. backboardd keeps swapping the progress UI (3700+ swaps in ~10 min, `SWEET FB: N swaps`). Sample during migration: SpringBoard main thread blocked in a synchronous XPC call (`mach_msg2_trap <- dispatch_mach_send_and_wait_for_reply <- xpc_connection_send_message_with_reply_sync <- -[BSXPCServiceConnectionMessage _sendSynchronously:] <- BSXPCServiceConnectionProxy ...`). Unsupported AKS selectors seen: 101 `aks_assert_consume`, 93 `aks_get_internal_info`, 34 `_aks_stash_load` (datamigrator), **35 `aks_get_extended_device_state`** (SpringBoard, chronod, remindd, searchd, symptomsd...). **Patch 0055**: AKS 35 answered like 17 (same DER device state), 101 succeeds; sampler backtraces 16 frames, 40 samples (~80 min).
- **MILESTONE: FIRST iOS PIXELS ON THE PANEL (boot 0053s long run, 15:33 UTC, ~60 min; photos IMG_20261017_183029/183222/183419; log `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/logs/boot-0053s-long.txt`, 407 KB, 1428 flushes).** The iOS data-migration boot screen (Apple logo + progress bar) was shown and the bar advanced to full in ~60 minutes. It is drawn by **backboardd through our kernel display path**: `SWEET SURF: backboardd create id 2/3 1080x2400 fmt 0x42475241 (BGRA) bpe 4 bpr 4352 size 10452992 keys 11`, IOSurface sels 9, 27, 5, 34 and traps 2/3 (lock/unlock), IOMFB SwapBegin (4) / SwapEnd (5, 1132-byte struct) / GetLayerDefaultSurface (3) / 68 / 55, then `SWEET FB: swap N layer 0 surface 2|3 1080x2400 ... a 0,0 1080x2400 b 0,0 1080x2400` alternating (double buffering; only the first 16 swaps are logged). Triggered by `com.apple.datamigrator` (AKS sels 101/93/34 return unsupported) and `com.apple.migrationpluginwrapper`. Probably ProgressUI-style CoreGraphics drawing into IOSurfaces (no GPU used). After the migration: SpringBoard and backboardd stay up (exec once each, no crash), nothing new drawn; crash loops only for mediaserverd, installcoordinationd, mobileactivationd (~108 each in the hour); bluetoothd and fairplayd.H2 relaunch constantly (no hardware). Kernel console text overlays the image (sweetfb_noconsole=1 would hide it).
- **MILESTONE: SpringBoard and backboardd both stay up (boot 0053s on v9, 15:29 UTC, `logs/boot-0053s.txt`).** With /chosen/display-scale = 3: `exec SpringBoard` once, `exec backboardd` once, no CRASH for either; MobileGestaltHelper reads IOMFB size/dot pitch; backboardd opens AppleCLCD (sels 57,15,22,29,33,79,74 as before). No IOSurface creates and no swaps: backboardd keeps polling `IOProviderClass IOAcceleratorES` (-> 0 found) and AGXAccelerator/IOAcceleratorESLegacy - no GPU, so no CoreAnimation renderer yet. Log goes quiet after ~47 s (only 47 flushes): the system idles. NEXT WALL: CoreAnimation rendering without a GPU (CPU path) so the window server produces frames.
- **SpringBoard's invalid mode = scale 0 (boot 0053n, 15:19 UTC, `logs/boot-0053n.txt`, heap scan):** `FBSDisplayMode cannot be configured as requested : <FBSDisplayMode: ...; infxinf@0x (1080x2400/0) 60Hz sRGB SDR>` - pixel size and 60 Hz are fine, scale is 0. libMobileGestalt (MGIOMFBSupport.c, sub_19c560a54) reads `display-scale` (u32, default 0) and `display-rotation` from **IODeviceTree:/chosen**, pixel size via IOMobileFramebufferGetDisplaySize (MobileGestaltHelper sel 8/28 in the log). mkadt now adds `/chosen/display-scale` = 3 (1080x2400 @3x = 360x800 pt). ADT `shim/out/sweet-0054hi.adt` (QEMU `research/q0054-hi.adt`), image `shim/out/shim-boot-0053s.img` = 0053 kernel + 0054hi ADT. Possible catch: MobileGestalt may have cached scale 0 in `/private/var/containers/Shared/SystemGroup/systemgroup.com.apple.mobilegestaltcache/...`.
- **MILESTONE: backboardd survives and opens the display (boot 0052n on v9, 15:07 UTC, `logs/boot-0052n.txt`).** No `CRASH backboardd`. It walks `IODeviceTree:/arm-io` children, matches IOMobileFramebuffer, `open AppleCLCD type 0 -> 0x0`, calls IOMFB selectors 57, 15, 22, 29, 33, 79 (GetBlock, out 1580/40), 74 (SupportedFrameInfo), 12, 6, 27, 17, 84, 50; opens IOCoreSurfaceRoot (sel 13); looks for AGXAccelerator / IOAcceleratorES / IOAcceleratorESLegacy (none, repeatedly), `IODeviceTree:/product` (none, repeatedly), `/buttons`, aop-smart-cover, IOPMrootDomain. No swaps yet. **SpringBoard now gets past display discovery** and dies (sig 5) in `-[FBSDisplayMode _initWithWidth:height:preferredScale:scaleOverride:refreshRate:gamut:hdr:rotation:virtual:validityCheck:]+580` <- `_initWithCADisplayMode:scale:rotation:` <- `FBSDisplayConfiguration _initWithImmutableDisplay:` <- `FBSDisplaySource initWithDisplay:` <- `FBSDisplayMonitor` (slide 0x14504000): "FBSDisplayMode cannot be configured as requested". Checks: width/height >= 2, preferredScale > 0, scaleOverride >= 0, refreshRate non-zero, valid gamut/HDR/rotation. Suspects: refresh rate 0 (IOMFB timing blocks answered with zeros) and/or scale 0 (MobileGestalt main-screen-scale; no /product node in the ADT). **Patch 0053**: heap text scan also on SIGTRAP and for SpringBoard (default sweetscan list backboardd,SpringBoard) to read the exact mode description.
- **Image v9 (2026-10-17) = v8 + `/private/var/containers/{Bundle,Data/System,Shared/SystemGroup,Temp}` (root:wheel 0755; on iOS these live on the data volume, absent from the rootfs) + launchd.plist with StandardOutPath/StandardErrorPath renamed to ...PatX (the in-place phone fix; daemons' stdout reaches the log via the 0048 /dev/null tee).** `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-ipsw/ios16-root-20H392-v9.img` SHA256 aa9f9139...6e67, `.simg` 12,145,303,864 bytes; written through `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,force`. **Patch 0052** (`xnu/patches/0052-sweet-hfs-rename-swap.patch`, CI `39042761826` -> `xnu/out-0052`, QEMU OK, image `shim/out/shim-boot-0052n.img`): vn_rename falls back to VNOP_EXCHANGE when VNOP_RENAMEX(RENAME_SWAP) returns ENOTSUP for two regular files on one volume (HFS exchange swaps data and cnode descriptors = swap semantics). Flashing v9 started 2026-10-17 ~15:00 UTC.
- **backboardd EXCEPTION FOUND (boot 0051n, 14:41 UTC, `logs/boot-0051n.txt`, patch 0051 heap scan):** `*** Terminating app due to uncaught exception 'NSInvalidArgumentException', reason: '*** -[NSURL initFileURLWithPath:]: nil string parameter'`. First-throw stack (slide 0x337f0000): `+[NSURL fileURLWithPath:]` <- `-[BSSystemContainerForCurrentProcessPathProvider libraryPath]` <- `cachesPath` <- backboardd main+0x1d858 (StartWindowServer, shader cache path). BaseBoard: libraryPath = `BSSystemSharedResourcesDirectoryForIdentifier` = `BSSystemSharedDirectoryForIdentifier(id)` + "/Library"; that calls `container_system_path_for_identifier` (libsystem_containermanager -> `_container_legacy_lookup` -> containermanagerd XPC), which returned NULL (BaseBoard logs "Error getting shared system container ..."). With 0050 the sandbox denials are gone. containermanagerd.log.0 in the pulled (pre-0050) image: "Did not receive any persona attributes", "Expected to find at least one persona", notify_register_dispatch failed 9; `/private/var/containers` does NOT exist in the root image (on iOS it lives on the data volume). usermanagerd does create kernel personas 99/199/1000, but fails to save `/private/var/keybags/usersession.kb` and `persona.kb`: "Failed to atomically swap writing file: Operation not supported (45)" (HFS has no RENAME_SWAP). NEXT: create `/private/var/containers` (and check what containermanagerd needs for system group containers), fix or emulate RENAME_SWAP on HFS, then re-check backboardd.

Boot 0051n 14:41 UTC.
- **Boot 0049n (14:11 UTC, `logs/boot-0049n.txt`, patch 0049 trace): backboardd's IOKit lookups before it dies** (each run): `IODeviceTree:/arm-io/pearl-sep` -> none, matching ApplePearlSEPDriver -> none, `IOService:/IOResources/AppleKeyStore` open OK, matching IOHIDDevice (DeviceUsagePairs) / `HIDServiceSupport` notifications, `usb-drd,t8027` notification; **never any IOMobileFramebuffer / display lookup** - the exception happens before display detection. launchd (tracev3) shows backboardd's mach lookups denied with `error = 159: Sandbox restriction`: com.apple.mobilegestalt.xpc, com.apple.iohideventsystem, com.apple.system.notification_center, com.apple.logd, containermanagerd, ACCHWComponentAuthService, oscard. libsystem_sandbox uses `__mac_syscall("Sandbox", op, arg)` (ops: 1 init, 2 check, 3 note, 4 container path, 5/6/7/8 extensions, 0x15 check_bulk, ...); with no policy named "Sandbox" every call gets ENOPOLICY. **Patch 0050** (`xnu/patches/0050-sweet-sandbox-policy.patch`): built-in permissive MAC policy "Sandbox" in sweet_amfi.c (mpo_policy_syscall returns 0, container/extension ops ENOENT; logs first use of each op as `SWEET Sandbox: proc op N`).
- **Phone root pulled (16 GiB, 12 min, `adb exec-out dd if=/dev/block/sda34`) to `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/phone-root.img` (loop-mounted ro at /run/media/dipa/iOSRoot).** No .ips crash reports. logd tracev3 (Persist, LZ4 `bv41` blocks, decompressed strings in `ios-sweet-work/tracev3/all.txt`): /private/var/db/uuidtext is EMPTY so only launchd's own messages are readable. Findings: v8 launch failure = `Service could not initialize: posix_spawn(/usr/libexec/backboardd), error 0xd - Permission denied` (xpcproxy exit 78) - /dev/console not openable for the mobile user; backboardd[98] spawned `com.apple.MTLCompilerService` before SIGABRT; **launchd denies many mach lookups with `error = 159: Sandbox restriction`** (mediaserverd -> com.apple.backboard.display.services, remoted, mobiletimerd -> sleepd, identityservicesd -> apsd) and **tccd exits OS_REASON_SANDBOX "profile not found"** - no Sandbox kext/profiles on SWEET; likely cause of many daemon crash loops (next wall after the display). CF's uncaught-exception text never reaches stderr for daemons (no CF handler; libc++abi prints only the type). **Patch 0049** (`xnu/patches/0049-sweet-iokit-trace.patch`): `IOK name: ...` console lines for registry path lookups, child iterators, matching (with result count/class), matching notifications and IOServiceOpen of the processes in boot-arg `sweetiolog` (default backboardd), max 400 lines.

It never opened AppleCLCD or IOCoreSurfaceRoot (no `SWEET FB/SURF: backboardd` lines) -> throws before/at display detection. **Patch 0048** (`xnu/patches/0048-sweet-devnull-tee.patch`, CI `39037749668` -> `xnu/out-0048`, image `shim/out/shim-boot-0048n.img`): writes to /dev/null by processes named in boot-arg `sweetnull` (default `backboardd,SpringBoard`) are printed as `|name| text`. Boot 0048n (13:48 UTC, log `logs/boot-0048n.txt`): only `|backboardd| libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type NSException` (CF's reason line goes to os_log, not stderr, despite CFLOG_FORCE_STDERR/OS_ACTIVITY_DT_MODE). Next: pull the whole 16 GiB root from recovery (`adb exec-out dd if=/dev/block/sda34`) to `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/phone-root.img` and read ReportCrash .ips files / logd tracev3 for the exception reason.
- **Persistent log WORKS (boot 0047n on v8, 13:31 UTC; pulled from OrangeFox: 70 KB, 111 flushes, `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/logs/boot-0047n.txt`).** In 3 min **backboardd was never exec'd** (v7 ran it), ~70 bare `exec xpcproxy`, SpringBoard loops on FBSDisplayMonitor (no display), mediaserverd sig 11 loop, locationd, lockdownd sig 5.
